GENERAL PART
Chapter One.
TASK AND LIMITS OF APPLICATION OF THE PENAL CODE

### Раздел I

Task of the Penal Code
Art. 1. (1) (Amended - SG, No. 1 of 1991) The Penal Code has the task of protecting the individual and the rights of citizens and the entire legal order established in the country from criminal encroachments.
(2) To accomplish this task, the Penal Code determines which socially dangerous acts are crimes and what punishments are imposed for them, and establishes the cases when, instead of punishment, measures of social impact and education may be imposed.

### Раздел II

Limits of application of the Penal Code
					Art. 2. (1) For every crime, the law that was in force at the time of its commission shall apply.
(2) If different laws follow before the sentence enters into force, the law that is most favourable to the perpetrator shall apply.
Art. 3. (1) The Penal Code applies to all crimes committed on the territory of the Republic of Bulgaria.
(2) The question of the liability of foreigners who enjoy immunity with respect to the criminal jurisdiction of the Republic of Bulgaria shall be resolved in accordance with the norms of international law accepted by it.
Art. 4. (1) The Penal Code applies to Bulgarian citizens also for crimes committed by them abroad.
(2) (Amended - SG, No. 75 of 2006, in force from 13.10.2006) A citizen of the Republic of Bulgaria may not be extradited to another state or to an international court for the purposes of criminal prosecution, unless this is provided for in an international treaty ratified, promulgated and entered into force for the Republic of Bulgaria.
Art. 5. The Penal Code also applies to foreigners who have committed crimes of a general nature abroad that affect the interests of the Republic of Bulgaria or of a Bulgarian citizen.
Art. 6. (1) The Penal Code also applies to foreigners who have committed a crime against peace and humanity abroad that affects the interests of another state or foreign citizens.
(2) The Penal Code also applies to other crimes committed by foreigners abroad when this is provided for in an international agreement in which the Republic of Bulgaria participates.
Art. 7. In the cases of Art. 4 and 5, the preliminary detention and the punishment served abroad shall be deducted. When the two punishments are of different kinds, the punishment served abroad shall be taken into account when determining the punishment by the court.
Art. 8. (1) (Previous text of Art. 8 - SG, No. 33 of 2011, in force from 27.05.2011) The sentence of a foreign court for a crime to which the Bulgarian Penal Code applies shall be taken into account in the cases established by an international agreement in which the Republic of Bulgaria participates.
(2) (New - SG, No. 33 of 2011, in force from 27.05.2011) A sentence that has entered into force, rendered in another Member State of the European Union, for an act that constitutes a crime under the Bulgarian Penal Code, shall be taken into account in any criminal proceedings conducted against the same person in the Republic of Bulgaria.
Chapter Two.
CRIME

### Section I

General Provisions
Art. 9. (1) A crime is that socially dangerous act (action or omission) which is committed culpably and is declared punishable by law.
(2) An act is not criminal which, although formally fulfilling the elements of a crime provided for by law, is not socially dangerous due to its insignificance or its social danger is manifestly negligible.
Art. 10. (Amended - SG, issue 50 of 1995) Socially dangerous is the act which threatens or harms the individual, the rights of citizens, property, the legal order established by the Constitution in the Republic of Bulgaria or other interests protected by law.
Art. 11. (1) A socially dangerous act is committed culpably when it is intentional or negligent.
(2) An act is intentional when the perpetrator was aware of its socially dangerous character, foresaw its socially dangerous consequences and desired or allowed the occurrence of these consequences.
(3) An act is negligent when the perpetrator did not foresee the occurrence of the socially dangerous consequences but was obliged and able to foresee them, or when he foresaw the occurrence of these consequences but thought to prevent them.
(4) Negligent acts are punishable only in cases provided for by law.
(5) When the law qualifies the act as a more serious crime due to the occurrence of additional socially dangerous consequences, if intent for these consequences is not required, the perpetrator is liable for the more serious crime when he acted negligently with respect to them.
Art. 12. (1) An act committed in necessary defence is not socially dangerous - in order to protect state or public interests, the person or rights of the defender or of another by causing harm to the attacker within the necessary limits.
(2) Exceeding the limits of necessary defence exists when the defence manifestly does not correspond to the nature and danger of the attack.
(3) (Amended - SG, issue 62 of 1997, amended - SG, issue 75 of 2006, effective from 13.10.2006) There is no exceeding the limits of necessary defence if the attack was carried out by penetrating with violence or by breaking into a dwelling.
(4) (Previous para. 3 - SG, issue 62 of 1997, amended - SG, issue 28 of 1982, effective from 01.07.1982) The perpetrator is not punished when he commits the act while exceeding the limits of necessary defence if this is due to fear or confusion.
Art. 12a. (New - SG, issue 62 of 1997) (1) Causing harm to a person who has committed a crime during his apprehension for handing over to the authorities and preventing the possibility of committing another crime is not socially dangerous, if there is no other way for his apprehension and if no exceeding of the necessary and lawful measures has been allowed during this apprehension.
(2) The necessary measures for apprehending a person who has committed a crime are exceeded when there is a manifest discrepancy between the nature and degree of social danger of the crime committed by the apprehended person and the circumstances of the apprehension, as well as when manifestly excessive harm is caused to the person without necessity. In these cases criminal liability is borne only in cases of intentional causing of the harm.
Art. 12b. (New - SG, issue 32 of 2010, effective from 28.05.2010) An act committed by a person who acted as an undercover officer within the scope of his powers determined by law is not socially dangerous.
Art. 13. (1) An act committed by someone in extreme necessity is not socially dangerous - in order to save state or public interests, as well as his own or another's personal or property interests from imminent danger which the perpetrator could not avoid in another way, if the harm caused by the act is less significant than that prevented.
(2) There is no extreme necessity when the very avoidance of the danger constitutes a crime.
Art. 13a. (New - SG, issue 28 of 1982, effective from 01.07.1982) (1) An act committed under justified economic risk is not socially dangerous - in order to achieve a substantial socially useful result or to avoid significant harm, if it does not contradict an express prohibition established by a normative act, corresponds to contemporary scientific and technical achievements and experience, does not endanger the life and health of another and the perpetrator has done everything depending on him to prevent the harmful consequences that occurred.
(2) In deciding whether the risk is justified, the relationship between the expected positive result and the possible negative consequences, as well as the probability of their occurrence, is also taken into account.
Art. 14. (1) Ignorance of the factual circumstances which belong to the elements of the crime excludes intent with respect to that crime.
(2) This provision also applies to negligent acts when the very ignorance of the factual circumstances is not due to negligence.
Art. 15. An act is not committed culpably when the perpetrator was not obliged or was not able to foresee the occurrence of the socially dangerous consequences (accidental act).
Art. 16. An act is not committed culpably which is carried out in execution of an unlawful official order given in the established manner, if it does not impose a crime that is obvious to the perpetrator.
Art. 16a. (1) (New - SG, issue 84 of 2013, previous text of Art. 16a - SG, issue 74 of 2015) An act is not committed culpably which is carried out by a person who suffered from trafficking in human beings when he was forced to carry it out in direct connection with this quality.
(2) (New - SG, issue 74 of 2015) An act is not committed culpably which is carried out by a minor who suffered from a crime under Art. 155, 156, 158a and Art. 188, para. 2, or by a minor used for the creation of pornographic material, when he was forced to carry it out in direct connection with this quality.

### Section II

Preparation and Attempt
Art. 17. (1) Preparation is the preparing of means, finding of accomplices and generally the creation of conditions for committing the intended crime, before its execution has begun.
(2) Preparation is punishable only in cases provided for by law.
(3) The perpetrator is not punished when of his own accord he has refused to commit the crime.
Art. 18. (1) An attempt is the commenced execution of an intentional crime in which the executive act has not been completed or although it has been completed, the socially dangerous consequences of that crime provided for by law and desired by the perpetrator have not occurred.
(2) In an attempt the perpetrator is punished with the punishment provided for the completed crime, taking into account the degree of realization of the intention and the reasons for which the crime remained uncompleted.
(3) In an attempt the perpetrator is not punished when of his own accord:
a) he has refused to complete the execution of the crime or
b) he has prevented the occurrence of the criminal consequences.
Art. 19. In the cases of Art. 17, para. 3 and 18, para. 3, if the act in which the preparation or attempt was expressed contains the elements of another crime, the perpetrator is liable for that crime.


This excerpt sits under: | Constitution | Regulations |

### Section III

Complicity
Art. 20. (1) Accomplices in the commission of an intentional crime are the perpetrators, instigators and accessories.
(2) A perpetrator is one who participates in the actual execution of the crime.
(3) An instigator is one who intentionally induced another to commit the crime.
(4) An accessory is one who intentionally facilitated the commission of the crime through advice, explanations, promise to provide assistance after the act, removal of obstacles, procurement of means or in another way.
Art. 21. (1) All accomplices shall be punished with the punishment provided for the committed crime, taking into account the nature and degree of their participation.
(2) The instigator and the accessory are liable only for that which they intentionally instigated or assisted the perpetrator to do.
(3) When by reason of a specific personal quality or relationship of the perpetrator the law elevates the committed act to a crime, both the instigator and the accessory are liable for that crime, even though these circumstances are not present for them.
(4) Special circumstances by reason of which the law excludes, reduces or increases the punishment for any of the accomplices shall not be taken into account for the other accomplices in respect of whom these circumstances are not present.
Art. 22. (1) The instigator and the accessory shall not be punished if of their own accord they withdraw from further participation and prevent the act from being committed or avert the occurrence of the criminal consequences
(2) In these cases the provision of Art.

### 19. Section IV

Plurality of Crimes
Art. 23. (1) If several crimes have been committed by one act or if one person has committed several separate crimes before there has been a final judgment for any of them, the court, after determining a punishment for each crime separately, shall impose the most severe of them.
(2) (Amended - SG, No. 92 of 2002, effective from 01.01.2005, amended regarding entry into force - SG, No. 26 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2004, amended - SG, No. 103 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2005) The imposed punishments of public reprimand and deprivation of rights under Art. 37, para. 1, items 6, 7 and 9 shall be added to the determined most severe punishment. If deprivation of identical rights has been imposed, the one of them which is for the longest term shall be imposed.
(3) When the punishments are different in kind and any of them is a fine or confiscation, the court may add it wholly or partially to the most severe punishment.
Art. 24. When the imposed punishments are of the same kind, the court may increase the determined overall most severe punishment by at most one half, but the punishment so increased may not exceed the sum of the individual punishments, nor the maximum amount provided for the respective kind of punishment.
Art. 25. (1) The provisions of Art. 23 and 24 shall also apply when the person has been convicted by separate judgments.
(2) In these cases, if the punishment under any of the judgments has been served wholly or partially, it shall be deducted if it is of the kind of the determined overall punishment to be served.
(3) (Amended - SG, No. 103 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2005) The served punishment of probation shall be deducted wholly from deprivation of liberty and vice versa, with two days of probation being counted as one day of deprivation of liberty.
(4) (New - SG, No. 28 of 1982, effective from 01.07.1982) When under one or more of the judgments the person has been released from serving the imposed punishment under the procedure of Art. 64, para. 1 or of Art. 66, the question of the execution of the overall punishment shall be decided upon its determination.
Art. 26. (Amended - SG, No. 62 of 1997, amended - SG, No. 92 of 2002) (1) The provisions of Art. 23 - 25 shall not apply in cases of continuing crime - when two or more acts which separately constitute one or different elements of one and the same crime have been committed over short periods of time, in one and the same situation and with homogeneity of guilt, whereby the subsequent ones appear from an objective and subjective perspective as a continuation of the preceding ones.
(2) In a continuing crime, the perpetrator shall be punished in accordance with the acts included in it, taken in their totality, and with the overall criminal result caused by them.
(3) When the individual acts constitute different elements, the continuing crime shall be punished under the more severe of them, taking into account the significance of the acts committed under aggravating circumstances and of the aggravating circumstances themselves for the overall criminal activity.
(4) When the aggravating circumstances do not significantly affect the severity of the overall criminal activity, the latter shall be subsumed under the lighter element, with the aggravating circumstances being taken into account in determining the punishment.
(5) When some of the acts are completed and others constitute an attempt and the completed acts do not significantly affect the nature of the overall criminal activity, the perpetrator shall be imposed a punishment as for an attempt.
(6) The provisions of this article shall not apply with respect to a crime against the person of different citizens and for crimes committed after the submission of the indictment for them to the court, as well as for crimes committed before the submission of the indictment but not included in it.
Art. 27. (1) (Amended - SG, No. 28 of 1982, effective from 01.07.1982) When a person commits a crime after having been convicted by a final judgment to deprivation of liberty but before having served that punishment, the court shall add to the unserved part wholly or partially the punishment under the second judgment, if it is deprivation of liberty. The determined overall punishment may not be less than the punishment under the second judgment.
(2) (Amended - SG, No. 28 of 1982, effective from 01.07.1982) The punishment under the second judgment shall be added wholly if it is deprivation of liberty of more than five years or if it has been imposed for a crime committed repeatedly or constituting dangerous recidivism.
(3) When the person has committed a crime after serving the punishment under the previous judgment, the imposed punishment for that crime shall be served wholly.
Art. 28. (1) The punishment provided in the special part of this code for a crime committed repeatedly shall be imposed if the perpetrator has committed a crime after having been convicted by a final judgment for another such crime.
(2) This provision shall also apply when it concerns identical kinds of crimes against public and personal property.
Art. 29. (1) The more severe punishments provided in the special part of this code for crimes constituting dangerous recidivism shall be imposed when the perpetrator:
a) (amended - SG, No. 28 of 1982, effective from 01.07.1982) commits the crime after having been convicted for a serious intentional crime to deprivation of liberty of not less than one year, the execution of which has not been suspended under Art. 66;
b) (amended - SG, No. 28 of 1982, effective from 01.07.1982) commits the crime after having been convicted two or more times to deprivation of liberty for intentional crimes of a general nature, if for at least one of them the execution of the punishment has not been suspended under Art. 66.
c) (repealed - SG, No. 28 of 1982, effective from 01.07.1982)
(2) In applying the provisions of the preceding paragraph, crimes committed by the perpetrator as a minor shall not be taken into account.
(3) (New - SG, No. 95 of 1975) When for a given crime elements for repeated commission and for dangerous recidivism are provided simultaneously and the act fulfills the features of both elements, the provision for dangerous recidivism shall apply.
Art. 30. (1) The rules of Art. 28 and 29 shall not apply if five years have elapsed from the serving of the punishment under the previous judgments. Rehabilitation within this term does not exclude their application.
(2) (New - SG, No. 28 of 1982, effective from 01.07.1982) In case of conditional conviction and conditional early release, the term under para. 1 shall begin to run from the day on which the probationary period has elapsed.
Chapter Three.
CRIMINALLY LIABLE PERSONS
Art. 31. (1) Criminally liable is an adult person - having reached 18 years of age - who in a state of sanity commits a crime.
(2) A minor - having reached 14 years but not having reached 18 years - is criminally liable if he or she has been able to understand the nature and significance of the act and to control his or her actions.
(3) (Amended - SG, No. 107 of 1996) Minors to whom the act cannot be imputed as guilt shall be placed by decision of the court in an educational boarding school or in another suitable institution if this is required by the circumstances of the case.
(4) With respect to the criminal liability of minors, the special rules provided in this code shall apply.
Art. 32. (1) Not criminally liable is an infant - not having reached 14 years of age.
(2) With respect to infants who have committed socially dangerous acts, appropriate educational measures may be applied.
Art. 33. (1) Not criminally liable is a person who acts in a state of insanity - when due to mental underdevelopment or prolonged or short-term disorder of consciousness he or she has not been able to understand the nature or significance of what was committed or to control his or her actions.
(2) (Amended - SG, No. 95 of 1975) No punishment shall be imposed on a person who has committed a crime when before the pronouncement of the judgment he or she falls into a disorder of consciousness as a result of which he or she cannot understand the nature or significance of his or her actions or control them. Such a person shall be subject to punishment if he or she recovers.
Art. 34. With respect to the persons indicated in the preceding article, in the cases provided in this code, appropriate compulsory medical measures may be applied.
Chapter Four.
PUNISHMENT


This excerpt sits under: | Constitution | Regulations |

### Раздел I

General Provisions
Чл. 35. (1) Criminal liability is personal.
(2) Punishment may be imposed only on a person who has committed a crime provided for by law.
(3) The punishment corresponds to the crime.
(4) Punishment for a crime is imposed only by the established courts.
Чл. 36. (1) The punishment is imposed with the purpose: 1) to reform and re-educate the convicted person towards observance of the laws and good morals, 2) to have a deterrent effect on him and to deprive him of the possibility to commit other crimes and 3) to have an educational and deterrent effect on other members of society.
(2) The punishment may not have the purpose of causing physical suffering or humiliation of human dignity.
(3) (New - SG, issue 153 of 1998) There is no death penalty in the Republic of Bulgaria.
Чл. 37. (1) The punishments are:
1. (new - SG, issue 50 of 1995) life imprisonment;
1а. (previous item 1 - SG, issue 50 of 1995) deprivation of liberty;
2. (new - SG, issue 92 of 2002, effective from 01.01.2005, amd. regarding entry into force - SG, issue 26 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2004) probation;
2а. (previous item 2 - SG, issue 92 of 2002, effective from 01.01.2005, amd. regarding entry into force - SG, issue 26 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2004, repealed - SG, issue 103 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2005)
3. confiscation of available property;
4. fine;
5. (repealed - SG, issue 92 of 2002, effective from 01.01.2005, amd. regarding entry into force - SG, issue 26 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2004)
6. deprivation of the right to hold a specific state or public position;
7. deprivation of the right to practice a specific profession or activity;
8. (repealed - SG, issue 92 of 2002, effective from 01.01.2005, amd. regarding entry into force - SG, issue 26 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2004)
9. deprivation of the right to received orders, honorary titles and distinctions;
10. deprivation of military rank;
11. public reprimand.
(2) (Amd. - SG, issue 153 of 1998) For the most serious crimes that threaten the foundations of the republic, as well as for other particularly dangerous intentional crimes, life imprisonment without replacement is provided as a temporary and exceptional measure.

### Section II

Types of Punishments
Art. 38. (Am. - SG, issue 153 of 1998) (1) The punishment of life imprisonment without substitution, provided in the special part for a given type of crime, shall be imposed only if the specifically committed crime is exceptionally grave and the objectives specified in Art. 36 cannot be achieved through a lighter punishment.
(2) The punishment of life imprisonment without substitution may not be imposed on a person who at the time of committing the crime had not reached twenty years of age, and with respect to military personnel, as well as in wartime - eighteen years of age. The punishment of life imprisonment without substitution may not be imposed on a woman who was in a state of pregnancy at the time of committing the crime or at the time of pronouncing the sentence.
Art. 38a. (New - SG, issue 50 of 1995) (1) Life imprisonment is compulsory isolation of the convicted person until the end of his life in places for serving the punishment of deprivation of liberty.
(2) Life imprisonment shall be imposed when the committed crime is exceptionally grave.
(3) Life imprisonment may be substituted with a punishment of deprivation of liberty for a term of thirty years, if the convicted person has served not less than twenty years.
(4) During the serving of the punishment of life imprisonment, working days are not credited.
(5) The served punishment of life imprisonment shall be credited towards deprivation of liberty.
Art. 39. (1) (Am. - SG, issue 28 of 1982, effective from 01.07.1982, am. - SG, issue 89 of 1986) Deprivation of liberty may be from three months to twenty years.
(2) (Para. 2 rep., former para. 3, am. - SG, issue 89 of 1986, am. - SG, issue 50 of 1995, am. - SG, issue 153 of 1998) By way of exception, the punishment of deprivation of liberty may be for a term of up to thirty years in case of substitution of life imprisonment for multiple crimes under Art. 24 and 27, para. 1, as well as for certain particularly grave intentional crimes in cases specifically indicated in the special part of this code.
Art. 40. (1) (Am. - SG, issue 28 of 1982, effective from 01.07.1982, am. - SG, issue 75 of 2006, effective from 13.10.2006, am. - SG, issue 32 of 2016) The punishment of deprivation of liberty shall be served in prisons, as well as in correctional homes and prison hostels attached to them.
(2) (Am. - SG, issue 89 of 1986, rep. - SG, issue 92 of 2002)
(3) Special care shall be taken with respect to young adults.
(4) (Suppl. - SG, issue 75 of 2006, effective from 13.10.2006) With respect to convicted persons with severe psychopathy or suffering from a disorder of consciousness that does not exclude sanity, as well as with respect to convicted persons who are dependent on narcotic substances, appropriate medical care shall be taken.
Art. 41. (1) The serving of the punishment of deprivation of liberty shall be accompanied by appropriate, correspondingly paid socially useful labour, through which the re-education of the convicted persons is aimed at, as well as the creation and enhancement of their professional qualifications.
(2) In addition, other measures for education and training shall be applied.
(3) The labour performed shall be credited towards reducing the term of the punishment, with two working days being counted as three days of deprivation of liberty.
(4) (Am. - SG, issue 28 of 1982, effective from 01.07.1982, am. - SG, issue 89 of 1986) When the convicted person, while serving the punishment of deprivation of liberty, systematically avoids socially useful labour, commits an intentional crime or serious violations of the established order and thereby shows that he is not being reformed, the court may revoke in whole or in part the crediting of working days from the last two years before the commission of the last violation.
(5) (Para. 5 rep., former para. 6 - SG, issue 89 of 1986) The procedure and manner for execution of the punishment of deprivation of liberty, including the special care under para. 3 of Art. 40, the payment of labour of the convicted persons, as well as their placement in employment after their release shall be regulated by law.
(6) (Am. - SG, issue 89 of 1974, former para. 7 - SG, issue 89 of 1986, am. - SG, issue 27 of 2009, effective from 01.06.2009, am. - SG, issue 13 of 2017, effective from 07.02.2017) The initial regime for serving the punishment of deprivation of liberty shall be determined by the court in accordance with the provisions of this code and of the special law.
Art. 42. (1) In wartime, the military court may postpone until the end of military operations the execution of the imposed punishment of deprivation of liberty by sending the convicted person to the active army. The postponement of execution may be revoked if the convicted person commits a new crime.
(2) Upon proposal of the commander, the court may fully or partially release the convicted person sent to the active army under the procedure of para. 1 from serving the imposed punishment, if he proves himself to be a good defender of the homeland.
(3) The court may also, without a proposal from the commander, release fully or partially from serving the imposed punishment a person discharged from the active army due to disability.
Art. 42a. (New - SG, issue 92 of 2002, effective from 01.01.2005, am. regarding entry into force - SG, issue 26 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2004, am. - SG, issue 103 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2005) (1) Probation is a set of measures for control and influence without deprivation of liberty, which are imposed together or separately.
(2) (Am. - SG, issue 103 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2005) The probation measures are:
1. mandatory registration at current address;
2. mandatory periodic meetings with a probation officer;
3. restrictions on free movement;
4. (am. - SG, issue 75 of 2006, effective from 13.10.2006) inclusion in courses for professional qualification, programmes for social influence;
5. correctional labour;
6. unpaid labour for the benefit of society.
(3) (Am. - SG, issue 103 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2005) The probation measures have a duration:
1. from 6 months to three years - for the measures under para. 2, items 1 - 4;
2. from three months to two years - for correctional labour;
3. from 100 to 320 hours per year for no more than three consecutive years - for unpaid labour for the benefit of society.
(4) (Am. - SG, issue 103 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2005) The measures under para. 2, items 1 and 2 shall be imposed mandatorily on all persons sentenced to probation, and the measures under para. 2, items 5 and 6 shall not be imposed on minors who have not reached 16 years of age.
(5) Probation shall be executed in a procedure determined by law.
Art. 42b. (New - SG, issue 103 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2005) (1) (Suppl. - SG, issue 27 of 2009, effective from 01.06.2009) The probation measure of mandatory registration at current address is the appearance and signing of the convicted person before the probation officer or an official designated by him in accordance with the periodicity determined by the court, but not less than twice a week.
(2) The probation measure of mandatory periodic meetings with a probation officer shall be conducted in the probation service on whose territory the current address of the convicted person is located. By way of exception, they may be conducted at another suitable place designated by the probation officer, if important reasons require this. The meetings are planned or extraordinary at the request of the probation officer or the convicted person.
(3) The probation measure of restrictions on free movement is the imposition of one or more of the following prohibitions on:
1. visiting precisely defined places, areas and establishments in the sentence;
2. leaving the settlement for more than 24 hours without permission from the probation officer or the prosecutor;
3. leaving the dwelling he inhabits for a specified period of the day.
(4) (Am. - SG, issue 75 of 2006, effective from 13.10.2006) The probation measure of inclusion in courses for professional qualification, programmes for social influence is aimed at labour integration or building social skills and abilities for lawful behaviour of the convicted person.
(5) The probation measure of unpaid labour is labour that is performed for the benefit of society without restricting the freedom of the convicted person.
Art. 43. (Am. - SG, issue 92 of 2002, am. - SG, issue 103 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2005) (1) The probation measure of correctional labour shall be executed at the workplace of the convicted person and includes deductions from his remuneration of 10 to 25 per cent for the benefit of the state. The time during which the measure is served shall not be credited as work experience.
(2) When the convicted person becomes unemployed, the court shall substitute the remainder of the correctional labour with unpaid labour for the benefit of society, with one day of the remainder being determined as one hour of unpaid labour. In this case, the term of unpaid labour may also be below the minimum under Art. 42a, para. 3, item 3.
(3) The provision of para. 2 shall also apply when the convicted person leaves the workplace where he is serving the punishment and does not notify the probation officer of his new workplace within one month.
(4) The time during which the deductions under para. 1 are not made shall not be credited towards the execution of the probation measure of correctional labour.
Art. 43a. (New - SG, issue 103 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2005) If the convicted person without justified reason does not comply with the imposed probation measure, upon proposal of the respective probation council, the court may:
1. impose another probation measure;
2. (suppl. - SG, issue 27 of 2009, effective from 01.06.2009) substitute in whole or in part the probation with deprivation of liberty, with two days of probation being substituted with one day of deprivation of liberty; in these cases the term of deprivation of liberty may also be below the minimum under Art. 39, para. 1.
Art. 44. (1) Confiscation is compulsory and gratuitous alienation for the benefit of the state of property belonging to the guilty person or of part of it, of certain properties of the guilty person or of parts of such properties.
(2) (Rep. - SG, issue 62 of 1997)
Art. 45. (1) Confiscation shall not be ordered if the guilty person does not possess available property that may be the subject of this punishment.
(2) Not subject to confiscation are the items necessary for the convicted person and his family for personal and domestic use, the items necessary for the exercise of his occupation, specified in a list adopted by the Council of Ministers, as well as the means for the maintenance of his family for one year.
Art. 46. In case of confiscation, the state shall be liable up to the amount of the value of the confiscated property for the restoration of damages caused by the crime, and thereafter also for the obligations of the convicted person arising before the initiation of criminal prosecution, when his remaining available property is not sufficient for the restoration of damages and for the payment of obligations.
Art. 47. (1) (Am. - SG, issue 28 of 1982, effective from 01.07.1982, am. - SG, issue 10 of 1993, am. - SG, issue 92 of 2002) The fine shall be determined in accordance with the property status, income and family obligations of the perpetrator, and in determining its amount the provisions of Chapter Five shall also be applied. It may not be less than 100 leva.
(2) The fine shall be collected from the property remaining from the convicted person, even after his death, if the sentence has entered into force before that.
(3) Items that are not subject to confiscation may not be sold for compulsory collection of the fine.
Art. 48. (Rep. - SG, issue 92 of 2002, effective from 01.01.2005, am. regarding entry into force - SG, issue 26 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2004)
Art. 49. (1) (Am. - SG, issue 92 of 2002, effective from 01.01.2005, am. regarding entry into force - SG, issue 26 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2004) The punishments of deprivation of rights under Art. 37, para. 1, items 6 and 7, when imposed independently or with another punishment not connected with deprivation of liberty, shall be ordered for a definite term of up to three years within the limits established in the special part of this code.
(2) (Am. - SG, issue 54 of 1978) When deprivation of such a right is imposed together with deprivation of liberty, its term may exceed the term of the latter by no more than three years, unless otherwise provided in the special part of this code.
(3) The term shall begin to run from the entry into force of the sentence, but the convicted person may not enjoy the rights of which he has been deprived before he has served the punishment of deprivation of liberty.
(4) The term of deprivation of rights shall be reduced by as much time as the term of deprivation of liberty has been reduced due to pardon, work or deduction of pre-trial detention.
(5) (Am. - SG, issue 153 of 1998) A person sentenced to life imprisonment without substitution shall be deprived of the rights indicated in the sentence forever.
Art. 50. (1) The punishments of deprivation of the right to hold a certain state or public office and deprivation of the right to exercise a certain profession or activity shall be imposed in the cases provided by law, if holding the respective office or exercising the respective profession or activity is incompatible with the nature of the committed crime.
(2) (New - SG, issue 28 of 1982, effective from 01.07.1982, rep. - SG, issue 92 of 2002, effective from 01.01.2005, am. regarding entry into force - SG, issue 26 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2004)
(3) (Former para. 2 - SG, issue 28 of 1982, effective from 01.07.1982) The punishments of deprivation of the right to received orders, honorary titles and distinctions and deprivation of military rank may be imposed only upon conviction for grave crimes.
Art. 51. After the expiration of the term, the convicted person may again exercise the rights of which he was deprived by the sentence. This does not apply to the rights under Art. 37, para. 1, items 9 and 10, which may be acquired again only by the procedure established for that purpose.
Art. 52. The punishment of public reprimand consists of public censure of the guilty person, which is announced before the respective collective, through the press or in another appropriate manner in accordance with what is indicated in the sentence.
Art. 53. (1) (Former text of Art. 53 - SG, issue 28 of 1982, effective from 01.07.1982) Regardless of criminal liability, the following shall be forfeited for the benefit of the state:
a) (suppl. - SG, issue 7 of 2019) items that belong to the guilty person and were intended for or served for the commission of an intentional crime. When the items are missing or have been alienated, their equivalent value shall be awarded;
b) items that belong to the guilty person and were the subject of an intentional crime - in cases expressly provided in the special part of this code.
(2) (New - SG, issue 28 of 1982, effective from 01.07.1982) The following shall also be forfeited for the benefit of the state:
a) items that are the subject or means of the crime, the possession of which is prohibited, and
b) (am. - SG, issue 7 of 2019) the direct and indirect benefit acquired through the crime, if they are not subject to return or restitution; when the benefit is missing or has been alienated, its equivalent value shall be awarded.
(3) (New - SG, issue 7 of 2019) Within the meaning of para. 2, letter "b":
1. direct benefit is any economic advantage that occurred as a direct consequence of the crime;
2. indirect benefit is any economic advantage that occurred as a result of disposal of the direct benefit, as well as any property obtained as a result of subsequent full or partial transformation of direct benefit, including when it has been mixed with property acquired from lawful sources; subject to forfeiture is the property up to the value of the included direct benefit together with the increases in the property that have occurred, if they are directly related to the disposal or transformation of the direct benefit and the inclusion of the direct benefit in the property.
Chapter Five.
DETERMINATION OF PUNISHMENT
Art. 54. (1) The court shall determine the punishment within the limits provided by law for the committed crime, being guided by the provisions of the general part of this code and taking into account:
the degree of social danger of the act and the perpetrator,
the motives for committing the act and the other mitigating and aggravating circumstances of guilt.
(2) Mitigating circumstances determine the imposition of a lighter punishment, and aggravating circumstances - of a heavier punishment.
Art. 55. (1) In case of exceptional or numerous mitigating circumstances, when even the lightest punishment provided by law proves to be disproportionately severe, the court:
1. determines the punishment below the lowest limit;
2. substitutes:
a) (am. - SG, issue 153 of 1998) life imprisonment - with deprivation of liberty from fifteen to twenty years;
b) (am. - SG, issue 28 of 1982, effective from 01.07.1982, am. - SG, issue 10 of 1993, am. - SG, issue 62 of 1997, am. and suppl. - SG, issue 92 of 2002, effective from 01.01.2005, am. regarding entry into force - SG, issue 26 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2004, am. - SG, issue 103 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2005) deprivation of liberty, when the lowest limit is not provided - with probation, and for minors - with probation or public reprimand;
c) (am. - SG, issue 28 of 1982, effective from 01.07.1982, am. - SG, issue 10 of 1993, am. - SG, issue 62 of 1997, am. - SG, issue 92 of 2002, effective from 01.01.2005, am. regarding entry into force - SG, issue 26 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2004, am. - SG, issue 103 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2005) probation - with a fine from one hundred to five hundred leva.
(2) In the cases of item 1 of the preceding paragraph, when the punishment is a fine, the court may go below the lowest limit by no more than one half.
(3) In these cases, the court may not impose the lighter punishment that the law provides alongside the punishment of deprivation of liberty.
(4) (Deleted - SG, issue 28 of 1982, effective from 01.07.1982)
Art. 56. Circumstances that have been taken into account by the law in determining the respective crime are not mitigating and aggravating circumstances.
Art. 57. (1) When in the special part of this code the possibility is provided for the committed crime to be imposed one of two or more punishments, the court shall determine the most appropriate punishment by type and amount, being guided by the rules of the preceding articles.
(2) When in the special part of this code the possibility is provided for a given crime to be imposed simultaneously two or more punishments, the court, being guided by the rules of the preceding articles, shall determine the amount of each one of them so that they in their totality correspond to the objectives specified in Art. 36.
Art. 58. The court may apply the provisions of Art. 55 also in the following cases:
a) in case of attempt - due to the incompleteness of the crime, taking into account also the circumstances under Art. 18, para. 2;
b) in case of aiding - when the degree of participation of the perpetrator in the crime is small.
Art. 58a. (New - SG, issue 27 of 2009, am. - SG, issue 26 of 2010) (1) When pronouncing a conviction sentence in the cases under Art. 373, para. 2 of the Criminal Procedure Code, the court shall determine the punishment of deprivation of liberty, being guided by the provisions of the General Part of this code and shall reduce the punishment thus determined by one third.
(2) In the cases under Art. 57, para. 1, when the court determines as most appropriate by type the punishment of life imprisonment without substitution, it shall not impose it, and shall substitute life imprisonment with deprivation of liberty from twenty to thirty years.
(3) The court shall determine the amount of the punishment of deprivation of liberty within the limits of the lowest minimum amount and the highest maximum amount of the punishment of deprivation of liberty determined under the conditions of para. 2 and the punishment of deprivation of liberty provided in the Special Part of this code.
(4) In cases when simultaneously the conditions under para. 1 - 3 and the conditions of Art. 55 are present, the court shall apply only Art. 55, if it is more favourable for the perpetrator.
(5) The rules under para. 1 - 4 shall not apply to the punishments provided in the Special Part of this code under Art. 37, para. 1, items 2 - 11.
Art. 59. (1) (Am. - SG, issue 92 of 2002, effective from 01.01.2005, am. regarding entry into force - SG, issue 26 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2004, am. - SG, issue 103 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2005, am. - SG, issue 27 of 2009) The time during which the convicted person was detained or with respect to whom a measure of restraint of house arrest was taken shall be deducted in the execution of the punishment of deprivation of liberty or probation, as follows:
1. one day of detention shall be credited as one day of deprivation of liberty or as three days of probation;
2. two days of house arrest shall be credited as one day of deprivation of liberty or as two days of probation.
(2) (New - SG, issue 27 of 2009) Detention within the meaning of para. 1, in addition to the measure of restraint of detention in custody, is also any other detention under the procedure of the Criminal Procedure Code, the Law on the Ministry of Internal Affairs or another law, related to the crime for which the person has been convicted or was detained for execution of the punishment.
(3) (New - SG, issue 28 of 1982, effective from 01.07.1982, former para. 2 - SG, issue 27 of 2009) The provision of the preceding paragraph shall also apply when the convicted person was detained on charges of another crime, for which the proceedings were terminated or ended with an acquittal sentence, if with respect to the acts the provision of Art. 23, para. 1 could be applied.
(4) (New - SG, issue 28 of 1982, effective from 01.07.1982, am. - SG, issue 103 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2005, former para. 3 - SG, issue 27 of 2009, suppl. - SG, issue 95 of 2016) In the execution of the punishment of deprivation of rights under Art. 37, para. 1, items 6 and 7, the time during which for the same act the convicted person was deprived by administrative procedure or by the procedure of Art. 69a of the Criminal Procedure Code of the possibility to exercise these rights shall be deducted.
Chapter Six.
SPECIAL RULES FOR MINORS
Art. 60. The punishment of minors shall be imposed with the aim primarily of re-educating them and preparing them for socially useful labour.
Art. 61. (1) (Am. - SG, issue 89 of 1986, am. - SG, issue 75 of 2006, effective from 13.10.2006) With respect to a minor who has committed due to infatuation or recklessness a crime that does not represent great social danger, the prosecutor may decide not to initiate or to terminate the initiated pre-trial proceedings, and the court may decide not to be brought to trial or not to be convicted, if with respect to him educational measures under the Law on Combating the Antisocial Acts of Minors and Juveniles can be successfully applied.
(2) In these cases, the court may itself impose an educational measure, notifying the local commission for combating the antisocial acts of minors and juveniles thereof, or send the file to it for imposition of such a measure.
(3) (Am. - SG, issue 89 of 1986, am. - SG, issue 107 of 1996, am. - SG, issue 26 of 2004, am. - SG, issue 75 of 2006, effective from 13.10.2006) When the prosecutor decides not to initiate pre-trial proceedings or to terminate the initiated pre-trial proceedings, he shall send the file to the commission for imposition of an educational measure.
Art. 62. Only the following punishments may be imposed on minors:
1. deprivation of liberty;
1a. (new - SG, issue 92 of 2002, effective from 01.01.2005, am. regarding entry into force - SG, issue 26 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2004, am. - SG, issue 103 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2005) probation;
2. public reprimand;
3. (am. - SG, issue 103 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2005) deprivation of the right to exercise a certain profession or activity under Art. 37, para. 1, item 7.
Art. 63. (1) For minors, the punishments provided in the special part of this code shall be substituted:
1. (suppl. - SG, issue 50 of 1995, am. - SG, issue 153 of 1998) life imprisonment without substitution and life imprisonment - with deprivation of liberty from three to ten years;
2. deprivation of liberty for more than ten years - with deprivation of liberty up to five years;
3. deprivation of liberty for more than five years - with deprivation of liberty up to three years;
4. deprivation of liberty up to five years inclusive - with deprivation of liberty up to two years, but not more than provided by law;
5. (am. - SG, issue 92 of 2002, effective from 01.01.2005, am. regarding entry into force - SG, issue 26 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2004, am. - SG, issue 103 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2005, am. - SG, issue 75 of 2006, effective from 13.10.2006) the fine - with public reprimand;
6. (new - SG, issue 92 of 2002, effective from 01.01.2005, am. regarding entry into force - SG, issue 26 of 2004, effective from 01.01.2004) probation for minors who have not reached 16 years of age - with public reprimand.
(2) (Am. - SG, issue 28 of 1982, effective from 01.07.1982) For minors who have reached sixteen years of age, the punishments provided in the special part of this code shall be substituted:
1. (suppl. - SG, issue 50 of 1995, am. - SG, issue 153 of 1998) life imprisonment without substitution, life imprisonment and deprivation of liberty for more than fifteen years - with deprivation of liberty from five to twelve years;
2. deprivation of liberty for more than ten years - with deprivation of liberty from two to eight years.
(3) (Am. - SG, issue 28 of 1982, effective from 01.07.1982) Within the limits under the preceding paragraphs, the court shall determine the punishment in accordance with the rules of Chapter Five.
Art. 64. (1) (Am. - SG, issue 107 of 1996) When the determined punishment is deprivation of liberty of less than one year and its execution has not been postponed under Art. 66, the minor shall be released from serving it and the court shall place him in an educational boarding school or impose on him another educational measure provided in the Law on Combating the Antisocial Acts of Minors and Juveniles.
(2) (Am. - SG, issue 107 of 1996) Upon proposal of the prosecutor or of the respective local commission for combating the antisocial acts of minors and juveniles, the court may also after pronouncing the sentence substitute the placement in an educational boarding school with another educational measure.
(3) The rule of para. 1 shall not apply:
a) when the minor has committed a crime during the serving of the punishment of deprivation of liberty and
b) when he is convicted after having reached the age of majority.
(4) The rule of para. 1 shall also not apply in cases of repeated conviction, if the court finds that for the reform and re-education of the perpetrator it is necessary for him to serve the punishment of deprivation of liberty and when:
a) its term is not less than six months or
b) if the perpetrator has already served a punishment of deprivation of liberty.
Art. 65. (1) Minors until reaching the age of majority shall serve the punishment of deprivation of liberty in a correctional home.
(2) (Am. - SG, issue 75 of 2006, effective from 13.10.2006) After reaching the age of majority, they shall be transferred to a prison or to a prison hostel. For completion of their education or qualification, upon proposal of the pedagogical council, with permission of the prosecutor, they may be left in the correctional home until reaching twenty years of age.
Chapter Seven.
RELEASE FROM SERVING AN IMPOSED PUNISHMENT

### Section I

Suspended sentence
Art. 66. (1) (Amended - SG, No. 28 of 1982, effective 01.07.1982, corrected No. 31 of 1982, amended - SG, No. 92 of 2002, effective 01.01.2005, amended regarding entry into force - SG, No. 26 of 2004, effective 01.01.2004) When the court imposes a sentence of deprivation of liberty up to three years, it may postpone the execution of the imposed sentence for a period of three to five years, if the person has not been sentenced to deprivation of liberty for a crime of general character and if the court finds that in order to achieve the purposes of the punishment and above all for the correction of the convicted person, it is not necessary to serve the sentence.
(2) (Amended - SG, No. 92 of 2002, effective 01.01.2005, amended regarding entry into force - SG, No. 26 of 2004, effective 01.01.2004) The probationary period may not exceed the term of the imposed sentence of deprivation of liberty by more than three years.
(3) (Repealed - SG, No. 92 of 2002, effective 01.01.2005, amended regarding entry into force - SG, No. 26 of 2004, effective 01.01.2004)
(4) (New - SG, No. 28 of 1982, effective 01.07.1982, supplemented - SG, No. 75 of 2006, effective 13.10.2006) During the probationary period the convicted person is obliged to work or to study, unless he is obliged to undergo treatment.
Art. 67. (1) When postponing the execution of the sentence, the court may entrust to the respective public organization or labor collective, with their consent, the provision of educational care towards the convicted person during the probationary period.
(2) When such consent is lacking or when the court finds it necessary, it entrusts to a designated person the educational care for the conditionally convicted person. If the conditionally convicted person has his residence in another locality, this person is designated by the respective district court.
(3) (New - SG, No. 92 of 2002, effective 01.01.2005, amended regarding entry into force - SG, No. 26 of 2004, effective 01.01.2004, amended - SG, No. 27 of 2009, amended - SG, No. 67 of 2023) When the postponed sentence of deprivation of liberty is not less than six months, the court may order one or more of the probation measures under Art. 42a, para. 2 during the probationary period.
(4) (New - SG, No. 28 of 1982, effective 01.07.1982, repealed, former para. 3 - SG, No. 92 of 2002, effective 01.01.2005, amended regarding entry into force - SG, No. 26 of 2004, effective 01.01.2004) When postponing the execution of the sentence with respect to a minor, the court notifies the respective local commission, which organizes the provision of educational care.
(5) (Amended - SG, No. 95 of 1975, former para. 4 - SG, No. 28 of 1982, effective 01.07.1982) The general control regarding the educational care and the behavior of conditionally convicted persons is exercised by the district court at their place of residence.
(6) (Former para. 5 - SG, No. 28 of 1982, effective 01.07.1982) The procedure and manner for applying the provisions of the preceding paragraphs are regulated by law.
Art. 68. (1) If before the expiration of the probationary period determined by the court the convicted person commits another intentional crime of general character, for which, even after this period, he is imposed a sentence of deprivation of liberty, he also serves the postponed sentence.
(2) If under the conditions of para. 1 the convicted person commits a negligent crime, the court may order that the postponed sentence not be served or be served in full or in part.
(3) (Amended - SG, No. 28 of 1982, effective 01.07.1982, amended - SG, No. 92 of 2002, effective 01.01.2005, amended regarding entry into force - SG, No. 26 of 2004, effective 01.01.2004, amended - SG, No. 103 of 2004, effective 01.01.2005) If the conditionally convicted person does not fulfill without justified reason any of the probation measures determined for him under the procedure of Art. 67, para. 3, the court may, upon proposal of the probation council, replace it with another or order to serve in full or in part the postponed sentence of deprivation of liberty.
(4) (New - SG, No. 75 of 2006, effective 13.10.2006) If the conditionally convicted person without justified reason interrupts the treatment, the court orders to serve in full the postponed sentence of deprivation of liberty.
(5) (Amended - SG, No. 28 of 1982, effective 01.07.1982, former para. 4 - SG, No. 75 of 2006, effective 13.10.2006) Outside the cases under the preceding paragraphs, the postponed sentence is not served.
Art. 69. (1) With respect to a person conditionally convicted for a crime which he committed as a minor, the probationary period is from one to three years.
(2) With respect to such a person in the cases of para. 1 of the preceding article, the court may order that he be released in part or in full from serving the sentence whose execution was postponed.
Art. 69a. (New - SG, No. 28 of 1982, effective 01.07.1982, amended - SG, No. 103 of 2004, effective 01.01.2005, amended - SG, No. 75 of 2006, effective 13.10.2006) In the cases under Art. 68, para. 2, 3 and 5 and Art. 69, para. 2, if the convicted person during the probationary period commits a new crime of general character, for which he is imposed a sentence of deprivation of liberty, or continues not to fulfill without justified reason any of the probation measures determined for him under the procedure of Art. 67, para. 3, he serves the remaining part of the sentence.

### Section II

Early release
Art. 70. (1) (Amended - SG, issue 153 of 1998, supplemented - SG, issue 103 of 2004, in force from 01.01.2005, amended - SG, issue 27 of 2009, amended - SG, issue 13 of 2017, in force from 07.02.2017) The court may order conditional early release from serving the remaining part of the sentence of deprivation of liberty with respect to a convicted person who has provided evidence of his correction and has actually served:
1. not less than one half of the imposed sentence;
2. not less than two thirds of the imposed sentence in cases of dangerous recidivism.
(2) (Amended - SG, issue 92 of 2002, repealed - SG, issue 13 of 2017, in force from 07.02.2017)
(3) (Repealed - SG, issue 13 of 2017, in force from 07.02.2017)
(4) (Amended - SG, issue 92 of 2002, in force from 01.01.2005, amended regarding entry into force - SG, issue 26 of 2004, in force from 01.01.2004) The conditional early release also affects the term of the sentences of deprivation of rights under Art. 37, para. 1, points 6 and 7.
(5) (Amended - SG, issue 92 of 2002, in force from 01.01.2005, amended regarding entry into force - SG, issue 26 of 2004, in force from 01.01.2004) Upon conditional early release, the court may release the convicted person from serving the imposed sentence of deprivation of rights under Art. 37, para. 1, points 6 or 7.
(6) (Amended - SG, issue 28 of 1982, in force from 01.07.1982, supplemented - SG, issue 92 of 2002, in force from 01.01.2005, amended regarding entry into force - SG, issue 26 of 2004, in force from 01.01.2004, supplemented - SG, issue 103 of 2004, in force from 01.01.2005, amended - SG, issue 27 of 2009, amended - SG, issue 13 of 2017, in force from 07.02.2017) Upon conditional early release, a probationary period is established for the convicted person in the amount of the unserved part of the sentence. Within the probationary period, but for no more than three years, the court may order one of the probation measures under Art. 42a, para. 2, points 1 - 4, taking into account a report from the probation officer.
(7) (Supplemented - SG, issue 92 of 2002, in force from 01.01.2005, amended regarding entry into force - SG, issue 26 of 2004, in force from 01.01.2004) The person released early serves separately the unserved part of the sentence if during the probationary period he commits a new intentional crime for which a sentence of deprivation of liberty is provided or does not comply with the imposed probation. If during this period the person released early commits a negligent crime, the court may order that the deferred sentence not be served or be served in whole or in part.
(8) (Corrected - SG, issue 29 of 1968) In the cases of the preceding paragraph, the convicted person serves in full the sentence from which he was released under para. 5 of this article.
(9) The term for rehabilitation under Art. 86 in case of conditional early release begins to run from the moment of expiration of the probationary period.
Art. 71. (1) The court may release early a minor sentenced to deprivation of liberty if he has been corrected, after he has actually served not less than one third of the imposed sentence.
(2) With respect to a person convicted of a crime which he committed as a minor, after reaching the age of majority, the provisions of Art. 70 apply regarding the effect of early release.
Art. 72. (Repealed - SG, issue 92 of 2002, in force from 01.01.2005, amended regarding entry into force - SG, issue 26 of 2004, in force from 01.01.2004)
Art. 73. (1) (Amended - SG, issue 75 of 2006, in force from 13.10.2006) With respect to persons released early, the court assigns the organization of supervision and educational care for them during the probationary period to the respective commission, and for minors - to the respective local commission for combating antisocial manifestations of minors and juveniles.
(2) When necessary, the court assigns supervision and educational care to a specific public organization with its consent or to a specific person, notifying the supervisory or local commission thereof.
(3) The general control and guidance regarding educational care and the behavior of persons released early is exercised by the district court at their place of residence.
(4) The procedure and manner for applying the provisions of the preceding paragraphs are regulated by law.

### Section III

Pardon
Art. 74. (Supplemented - SG, issue 75 of 2006, in force from 13.10.2006) The President may, through pardon, remit in whole or in part the imposed sentence, and the death penalty, life imprisonment without commutation and life imprisonment - remit or commute.
Chapter Eight.
RELEASE FROM CRIMINAL LIABILITY

### Section I

Conditional release from criminal liability with transfer to public guarantee
Art. 75. (Repealed - SG, issue 62 of 1997)
Art. 76. (Repealed - SG, issue 62 of 1997)

### Section II

Release from criminal liability with imposition of measures for public influence by the comradely court
Art. 77. (Repealed - SG, issue 105 of 1991).

### Section III

Release from criminal liability of minors with application of educational measures
Art. 78. In the cases specified in Art. 61, the minor may be released from criminal liability by applying an appropriate educational measure.

### Section IV

Release from criminal liability with imposition of administrative punishment
Art. 78a. (New - SG, issue 28 of 1982, in force from 01.07.1982) (1) (Amended - SG, issue 10 of 1993, amended - SG, issue 62 of 1997, amended - SG, issue 21 of 2000, amended - SG, issue 75 of 2006, in force from 13.10.2006, amended - SG, issue 26 of 2010) An adult person is released from criminal liability by the court and is imposed a fine from one thousand to five thousand leva when the following conditions are simultaneously present:
a) (amended - SG, issue 86 of 2005, in force from 29.04.2006) for the crime a sentence of deprivation of liberty up to three years or another lighter sentence is provided when it is intentional, or deprivation of liberty up to five years or another lighter sentence when it is negligent;
b) the perpetrator has not been convicted of a crime of general character and has not been released from criminal liability under the procedure of this section;
c) the property damages caused by the crime have been restored.
(2) (Repealed - SG, issue 21 of 2000)
(3) (Repealed - SG, issue 21 of 2000)
(4) The court that imposes the fine under para. 1 may also impose an administrative punishment of deprivation of the right to exercise a certain profession or activity for a period of up to three years if deprivation of such a right is provided for the respective crime.
(5) When only a fine or a fine and another lighter sentence is provided for the committed crime, the administrative punishment may not exceed the amount of that fine.
(6) (New - SG, issue 26 of 2010) When the grounds under para. 1 are present and the act was committed by a minor, the court releases him from criminal liability by imposing an administrative punishment of public reprimand or an educational measure. The court may also impose an administrative punishment of deprivation of the right to exercise a certain profession or activity for a period of up to three years if deprivation of such a right is provided for the respective crime.
(7) (New - SG, issue 86 of 2005, in force from 29.04.2006, amended - SG, issue 75 of 2006, in force from 13.10.2006, supplemented - SG, issue 27 of 2009, previous para. 6 - SG, issue 26 of 2010, amended - SG, issue 95 of 2016, supplemented - SG, issue 54 of 2017, supplemented - SG, issue 67 of 2023) Paragraphs 1 - 6 do not apply if the caused injury is grievous bodily harm or death, or the perpetrator was in a state of intoxication, or after use of narcotic substances or their analogues, as well as in case of multiple crimes as well as when the crime was committed against an authority during or in connection with the performance of his service, except in cases when it is insult or slander.

### Section V

Determining the type of exemption from criminal liability
Art. 78b. (Repealed - SG, issue 62 of 1997)
Chapter Nine.
EXTINCTION OF CRIMINAL PROSECUTION AND OF THE IMPOSED PUNISHMENT
Art. 79. (1) Criminal prosecution and the execution of punishment are excluded:
1. when the perpetrator dies;
2. when the limitation period provided by law has expired;
3. when amnesty has followed.
(2) (Amended - SG, issue 74 of 2015 (*)) Criminal prosecution and the execution of punishment are not excluded by limitation in respect of:
1. crimes against peace and humanity;
2. (declared unconstitutional by Constitutional Court Decision No. 12 of 2016 - SG, issue 83 of 2016) serious crimes under Chapter Two, Section I, Section II, Section IV and Section V; Chapter Three, Section I, Section II and Section III; Chapter Eleven, Section III of the Special Part of the Criminal Code, committed in the period from 9 September 1944 to 10 November 1989 by members of the leading bodies of the Bulgarian Communist Party, as well as by third persons entrusted with leading official or party functions.
Art. 80. (1) Criminal prosecution is excluded by limitation when it has not been initiated within:
1. (amended - SG, issue 31 of 1990, amended - SG, issue 153 of 1998) twenty years for acts punishable by life imprisonment without commutation, life imprisonment, and 35 years for murder of two or more persons;
2. fifteen years for acts punishable by deprivation of liberty of more than ten years;
3. ten years for acts punishable by deprivation of liberty of more than three years;
4. (amended - SG, issue 62 of 1997) five years for an act punishable by deprivation of liberty of more than one year, and
5. (amended - SG, issue 26 of 2010) three years for all other cases.
(2) The limitation periods under the preceding paragraph for crimes committed by minors shall be determined after taking into account the substitution of punishments under Art. 63.
(3) The limitation period for prosecution begins from the completion of the crime, in case of attempt and preparation - from the day when the last act was committed, and for crimes that continue uninterruptedly, as well as for continuing crimes - from their termination.
Art. 81. (1) The limitation period is suspended when the commencement or continuation of criminal prosecution depends on the resolution of a preliminary question by a judicial act that has entered into force.
(2) The limitation period is interrupted by any action of the competent authorities undertaken for prosecution, and only in respect of the person against whom the prosecution is directed. After the completion of the action by which the limitation period was interrupted, a new limitation period begins to run.
(3) Regardless of the suspension or interruption of the limitation period, criminal prosecution is excluded if a period has elapsed that exceeds by one half the period provided in the preceding article.
Art. 82. (1) The imposed punishment shall not be executed when the following periods have elapsed:
1. (amended - SG, issue 153 of 1998) twenty years, if the punishment is life imprisonment without commutation or life imprisonment;
2. fifteen years, if the punishment is deprivation of liberty of more than ten years;
3. ten years, if the punishment is deprivation of liberty from three to ten years;
4. five years, if the punishment is deprivation of liberty of less than three years, and
5. two years for all other cases.
(2) The limitation period for execution of punishment begins to run from the day when the sentence entered into force, and in respect of punishment whose execution has been postponed pursuant to Art. 66 - from the entry into force of the sentence or the ruling under Art. 68.
(3) The limitation period is interrupted by any action of the competent authorities undertaken in respect of the convicted person for execution of the sentence. After the completion of the action by which the limitation period is interrupted, a new limitation period begins to run.
(4) Regardless of the suspension or interruption of the limitation period, the punishment shall not be executed if a period has elapsed that exceeds by one half the period provided in paragraph 1.
(5) (New - SG, issue 28 of 1982, in force from 01.07.1982) The provision of the preceding paragraph shall not apply in respect of a fine when enforcement proceedings have been instituted for its collection.
Art. 83. Amnesty erases the criminal character of a certain type of committed acts or exempts from criminal liability and from the consequences of conviction for certain crimes.
Art. 84. (1) For crimes that are prosecuted upon complaint of the injured party, criminal prosecution shall not be initiated, even if the limitation period has not expired, if a complaint is not filed within a six-month period from the day when the injured party learned that the crime was committed.
(2) If the injured party dies before this period has expired, the complaint may be filed by his heirs until its expiration.
(3) For these crimes, the punishment shall not be executed if the complainant has requested this before the commencement of its execution.
Chapter Ten.
REHABILITATION
Art. 85. (1) (Previous text of Art. 85 - SG, issue 28 of 1982, in force from 01.07.1982) Rehabilitation erases the conviction and cancels for the future the consequences that the laws associate with the conviction itself, unless in some respect a law or decree has established otherwise.
(2) (New - SG, issue 28 of 1982, in force from 01.07.1982) The provision of the preceding paragraph shall not apply in respect of persons convicted of crimes against peace and humanity.
Art. 86. (1) Rehabilitation occurs by operation of law in the following cases:
1. when the person has been conditionally convicted, if during the probationary period he has not committed another crime for which he must serve the postponed punishment;
2. (amended - SG, issue 92 of 2002, in force from 01.01.2005, amended regarding entry into force - SG, issue 26 of 2004, in force from 01.01.2004, amended - SG, issue 103 of 2004, in force from 01.01.2005) when convicted to deprivation of liberty up to three years or to probation, if within three years from the expiration of the term of the punishment imposed by the sentence or reduced by work or pardon, he has not committed another crime punishable by deprivation of liberty or by a more severe punishment;
3. when convicted jointly or separately to a fine, public reprimand or deprivation of rights, if within one year from the execution of the punishment he has not committed another crime of a general character, and
4. when convicted as a minor, if within two years from serving the punishment he has not committed another crime of a general character for which he was imposed a punishment of deprivation of liberty.
(2) (Amended - SG, issue 28 of 1982, in force from 01.07.1982) Rehabilitation by operation of law does not occur for a crime committed by an adult who has been rehabilitated once.
Art. 87. (1) Outside the cases under the preceding article, any convicted person may be rehabilitated by the court that issued the sentence as a first instance, if within three years from the expiration of the term of the punishment imposed by the sentence or reduced by work or pardon he has not committed another crime punishable by deprivation of liberty or by a more severe punishment:
1. if he has had good conduct and
2. if in case of an intentional crime he has restored the damages caused.
(2) The court may rehabilitate the convicted person even without having restored the damages caused, if there are valid reasons for this.
(3) (Amended - SG, issue 92 of 2002, in force from 01.01.2005, amended regarding entry into force - SG, issue 26 of 2004, in force from 01.01.2004, amended - SG, issue 103 of 2004, in force from 01.01.2005) When along with the punishment of deprivation of liberty a punishment of deprivation of rights under Art. 37, para. 1, points 6 and 7, or probation has also been imposed, in order for rehabilitation to be granted, the term of that punishment must have elapsed. When a fine has been imposed, it must be paid.
Art. 88. Rehabilitation may also be requested by the heirs of the convicted person after his death, if he had the right to it.
Art. 88a. (New - SG, issue 28 of 1982) (1) (Supplemented - SG, issue 89 of 1986) When from the serving of the punishment a period has elapsed equal to that under Art. 82, para. 1 and the convicted person has not committed a new intentional crime of a general character for which a punishment of deprivation of liberty is provided, the conviction and its consequences are erased regardless of what is provided in another law or decree.
(2) When the imposed punishment of deprivation of liberty is more than one year and the person has not been released from serving it on the basis of Art. 66, the period under para. 1 may not be less than ten years.
(3) In case of conditional conviction and conditional early release, the period under para. 1 begins to run from the day on which the probationary period has expired.
(4) (New - SG, issue 89 of 1986) When the person has committed two or more crimes for which he has not been rehabilitated, the conviction and its consequences are erased after the expiration of the periods provided in the preceding paragraphs for all convictions.
(5) (Previous para. 4 - SG, issue 89 of 1986) The provisions of the preceding paragraphs shall not apply in respect of persons convicted of serious crimes against the Republic and of crimes against peace and humanity.
Chapter Eleven.
COMPULSORY MEDICAL MEASURES
Art. 89. In respect of a person who has committed a socially dangerous act in a state of insanity or who has fallen into such a state before the pronouncement of the sentence or during the serving of the punishment, the court may order:
a) handing over to relatives, if they undertake an obligation for his treatment under the supervision of a psycho-neurological dispensary;
b) compulsory treatment in an ordinary psycho-neurological institution;
c) compulsory treatment in a special psychiatric hospital or in a special ward in an ordinary psycho-neurological institution.
Art. 90. (1) Compulsory treatment in an ordinary psycho-neurological institution may be ordered by the court in respect of a mentally ill person who, in view of his mental state and the nature of the socially dangerous act committed, needs hospital care and treatment by compulsory order.
(2) Compulsory treatment in a special psychiatric hospital or in a special ward may be ordered by the court in respect of a mentally ill person who, in view of his mental state and the nature of the socially dangerous act committed, appears particularly dangerous to society or to his relatives. In these cases the person is kept under enhanced supervision, excluding the possibility that he may commit a new socially dangerous act.
Art. 91. (1) The termination and modification of the ordered compulsory medical measure is made by the court when this is necessitated by a change that has occurred in the condition of the patient or by the needs of his treatment.
(2) In all cases, after the expiration of a six-month period from the placement in the medical institution, the court pronounces on the termination, continuation or replacement of the compulsory treatment.
Art. 92. (1) When the crime has been committed by a person suffering from alcoholism or from another drug addiction, the court may order compulsory treatment along with the punishment.
(2) When a punishment without deprivation of liberty has been imposed, the compulsory treatment is carried out in medical institutions with a special therapeutic and labor regime.
(3) The compulsory treatment of persons convicted to deprivation of liberty is conducted during the execution of the punishment. The period of compulsory treatment is deducted from the term of deprivation of liberty.
(4) When this is necessary, the court may order that the treatment continue even after the release of the convicted person from the place of deprivation of liberty - in the medical institutions indicated in paragraph two.
(5) Compulsory treatment is terminated by the court when its continuation is no longer necessary.
Supplementary Provision
Explanation of certain words
Art. 93. The words and expressions indicated below are used in this code in the following sense:
1. "Official" is one who is entrusted to perform with salary or without payment, temporarily or permanently:
a) service in a state institution with the exception of those performing activity only of material execution;
b) (amended - SG, issue 10 of 1993, amended - SG, issue 62 of 1997, supplemented - SG, issue 43 of 2005, in force from 01.09.2005, amended - SG, issue 64 of 2007, amended - SG, issue 26 of 2010) managerial work or work related to guarding or managing another's property in a state enterprise, cooperative, public organization, other legal entity or with a sole trader, as well as a notary and assistant notary, private bailiff and assistant private bailiff.
2. (amended - SG, issue 92 of 2002) "Authority" are the bodies of state power, the bodies of state administration, the bodies of the judicial power, as well as the employees with them who are charged with the exercise of authoritative functions.
3. "Representative of the public" is a person designated by a public organization to exercise on the basis of law or another normative act a specific function.
4. (supplemented - SG, issue 51 of 2000) "Public property" is the property of the state, of the municipalities, of the cooperatives, of the public organizations and of the other legal entities in which they participate.
5. "Official document" is one that is issued in the established order and form by an official within the scope of his service or by a representative of the public within the scope of the function entrusted to him.
6. "Inauthentic document" is one that has been given the appearance of representing a specific written statement of another person, and not of the one who actually composed it.
7. (supplemented - SG, issue 50 of 1995, amended - SG, issue 153 of 1998) "Serious crime" is one for which the law provides a punishment of deprivation of liberty of more than five years, life imprisonment or life imprisonment without commutation.
8. "Particularly serious case" is one in which the crime committed, in view of the harmful consequences that have occurred and other aggravating circumstances, reveals an exceptionally high degree of social danger of the act and the perpetrator.
9. "Minor case" is one in which the crime committed, in view of the absence or insignificance of the harmful consequences or in view of other mitigating circumstances, represents a lower degree of social danger compared to the ordinary cases of a crime of the respective type.
10. "Relatives" are spouses, ascendants, descendants (including adopted, stepchildren and found), brothers, sisters and their spouses, relatives in the collateral line up to IV degree.
11. "Wartime" is the time from the declaration of war or from the actual commencement of military actions until the announcement of their termination.
